# Proposed OEIS comments: A253700 to A259521

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A253700

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A253700>  
Result obtained: **5 September 2026**  
Paper: [6692](../papers/06501-07000/06692-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-2) +2*a(n-4) +2*a(n-5) -3*a(n-6) +a(n-7) -a(n-8) -6*a(n-9) +2*a(n-10) -2*a(n-12) -3*a(n-13) +a(n-14) +6*a(n-15) +a(n-16) +a(n-17) -a(n-18) -a(n-19) for n>21

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 121 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 21. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 234 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 117 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 50, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A259216

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A259216>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3653](../papers/03501-04000/03653-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 9 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A259220

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A259220>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3285](../papers/03001-03500/03285-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 129 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A259457

Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [10242](../papers/10001-10500/10242-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: 3*n*a(n) +(-3*n^2-19*n-44)*a(n-1) -2*(n+2)^2*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 15 2015

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A259513

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A259513>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10716](../papers/10501-11000/10716-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 73 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
